# Agents · K · part 1

891 entries, k to kod-gozden-gecirici. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **k**: Strategic advisor. Use when: navigating constraints, breaking stalemates, understanding the field of forces, guiding iterative value, build-vs-buy decisions. &lt;example&gt; Context: Team is stuck between competing concerns. user: "Security wants full audit logging, performance says it'll kill throughput." assistant: "I'll invoke K to find the strategic path through." &lt;commentary&gt; Competing forces creating stalemate. K finds the move that creates options. &lt;/commentary&gt; &lt;/example&gt; &lt;example&gt; Context: Architecture decision with many stakeholders. user: "Platform team wants standardization, product wan… ([yzavyas/claude-1337](https://github.com/yzavyas/claude-1337/blob/HEAD/plugins/arch-guild/agents/k.md) · ★8)
- **k-exaone**: Use when the main Claude thread wants a second pass from K-EXAONE 236B (LG AI Research), served by FriendliAI, via the opencode CLI — Korean-heavy prompts, Korean domain reasoning, long-context reading, or an independent opinion on a coding task. Do not use for trivial asks the main thread can finish on its own. ([kingsj0405/ClaudeCODEXaone](https://github.com/kingsj0405/ClaudeCODEXaone/blob/HEAD/plugins/exaone-friendli/agents/k-exaone.md) · ★3)
- **k-explorer**: Worker tier — task kinds `explore-search` and `summarize`. Use for read-only fan-out: find where something lives in code/docs, sweep many files, condense files/logs/docs into a brief. Returns conclusions, not file dumps. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-explorer.md) · ★7)
- **k-implementer**: Worker tier, mid rung — task kind `implement-under-spec`. Use to write code against a decided contract/design: the signatures, data shapes, and structure are already fixed; no open design decisions remain. Ships the change with its tests. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-implementer.md) · ★7)
- **k-mechanic**: Worker tier — task kinds `mech-edit`, `test-scaffold`, `doc-sync`. Use for mechanical, behaviour-preserving work from an explicit spec: renames, sweeps, formatting, moving code without reshaping it, test skeletons/fixtures from a stated spec, propagating an already-decided fact across docs. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-mechanic.md) · ★7)
- **k-reasoner**: Reasoner tier — task kinds `debug-deep`, `design-fork`, `quant-derivation`, `plan-draft`. Use for load-bearing thinking: multi-cause/cross-boundary failure analysis, drafting options for a contested design fork, deriving pricing/quant math, drafting a decomposition/zone plan for the orchestrator. Returns a structured report; it drafts, it never decides or edits. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-reasoner.md) · ★7)
- **k-validator**: Worker tier — task kind `validate-loop`. Use to drive project gates to green: run tests/lint/verification tools, parse the output, apply minimal mechanical fixes, re-run until green — or stop with a diagnosis when the failure is not mechanical. ([akumidv/alphavar](https://github.com/akumidv/alphavar/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/k-validator.md) · ★7)
- **k3-ask**: Use this agent when the user wants Kimi to answer a free-form question about the repository in prose — explain a module, trace a flow, compare alternatives, or reason about a concept in context. Choose this agent over k3-review when the user wants a narrative answer rather than diff-focused findings, and over k3-rescue when the user wants understanding rather than implementation. ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-ask.md))
- **k3-challenge**: Use this agent when Claude wants Kimi to run an adversarial review that challenges the implementation approach, design choices, tradeoffs, and assumptions rather than just flagging defects. Choose this agent when the user wants pushback on whether the chosen approach is the right one, not a tighter pass over implementation defects (see k3-review for that). ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-challenge.md))
- **k3-pursue**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked Kimi to pursue a stated objective AUTONOMOUSLY across multiple turns (experimental goal mode) — a multi-step task the user wants Kimi to drive to completion on its own, not a single bounded edit. This is the plugin's highest-autonomy surface: WRITE-CAPABLE, reusing the rescue trust boundary (the /k3:setup PreToolUse hook + workspace allowlist gate every tool call on every continuation turn; it cannot mutate git state), bounded by a MANDATORY hard --budget wall-clock ceiling. Requires BOTH an explicit objective AND explicit intent for hand… ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-pursue.md))
- **k3-rescue**: Use this agent proactively when the user wants to delegate a substantial investigation or implementation task to Kimi, especially multi-step bug hunts, cross-file refactors, or work the main Claude thread would rather offload than context-switch through. Choose this agent when the work is too large for an inline response but the user clearly wants it handed off, not just reviewed. ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-rescue.md))
- **k3-review**: Use this agent when Claude wants an independent second-pair-of-eyes review from Kimi over a working-tree diff or branch diff. Choose this agent for read-only diff review — multi-file changes, design-risk changes, or when the user explicitly asks for another reviewer. Not for implementation work (see k3-rescue) or free-form Q&A (see k3-ask). ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-review.md))
- **k3-swarm**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked for a READ-ONLY review fanned out in PARALLEL across many independent targets (files, modules, or questions) at once — a user-scoped whole-directory or repo-wide audit where one subagent per target is the point. Requires BOTH signals: many independent targets AND an explicit request for parallel fan-out. Do NOT auto-promote a single, unscoped, or diff-shaped review into a per-file swarm — use k3-review for a single working-tree/branch diff or any review the user did not scope as a fan-out. Read-only (every subagent is hook-denied writes —… ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-swarm.md))
- **k3-swarm-write**: Use this agent ONLY when the user has explicitly asked Kimi to make EDITS across MANY disjoint targets IN PARALLEL (a write fan-out) — e.g. "apply this same change to every handler, in parallel" or "fan out these independent edits across these N files." Requires BOTH signals: many independent WRITE targets AND explicit fan-out intent. WRITE-CAPABLE but PATCH-ONLY: edits happen in an ephemeral throwaway git worktree off HEAD and come back as a reviewable .patch — the plugin NEVER applies or commits, the user owns the merge, and the real working tree is never touched. Bounded by a MANDATORY har… ([Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc](https://github.com/Brilia-it/k3-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/k3-swarm-write.md))

- **k8s-security-reviewer**: Use this agent when you need to review Kubernetes configurations and architectures for security vulnerabilities and compliance. - **kb-concept-extractor**: Extracts domain concepts and terminology for concept_map.md from pre-filtered files ([rp1-run/rp1](https://github.com/rp1-run/rp1/blob/HEAD/plugins/base/agents/kb-concept-extractor.md) · ★38)
- **kb-content-distiller**: Writes the Markdown KB files from extracted LaTeX content and taxonomy skeleton positions. Two modes: Leaf (verbatim LaTeX→Markdown translation, no editorial changes) and Summary (progressive executive summary for domain/subtopic/entry-point levels). Parallel-execution safe — one domain per instance. ([ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core](https://github.com/ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/kb-content-distiller.md) · ★3)
- **kb-coordinator**: Orchestrates conversion of LaTeX source volumes into a hierarchical, hyperlinked Markdown knowledge base. Manages the full build pipeline: source survey, taxonomy design, extraction, distillation, validation, review loop, and meta-documentation. Use when constructing or significantly revising a KB from LaTeX sources. ([ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core](https://github.com/ave-veritas-et-enodatio/AVE-Core/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/kb-coordinator.md) · ★3)
- **kb-curation**: KB curation, enrichment, and citation specialist. Delegate when the task is to enrich entity/concept pages, repair citations and back-links, resolve contradictions, synthesize concepts, or maintain taxonomy, page templates, and resolver fixtures. Trigger phrases: "enrich this page", "fix citations", "audit provenance", "resolve this contradiction", "synthesize these concepts", "fix the filing / taxonomy", "update resolver fixtures". Not for retrieving answers (use kb-retrieval), ingesting new sources (use kb-ingestion), or read-only plugin/vault health audits (use kb-ops). ([weisberg/agile_agentic_analytics](https://github.com/weisberg/agile_agentic_analytics/blob/HEAD/plugins/knowledge-base/agents/kb-curation.md) · ★1)
- **kb-curator**: Keeps project memory and any registries fresh and honest — regenerates, checks staleness, flags uncited claims. ([amotion-ai/airx](https://github.com/amotion-ai/airx/blob/HEAD/agents/kb-curator.md) · ★8)
- **kb-curator**: Audits KB ingestion quality, checks for duplicate content in ChromaDB/Neo4j, validates graph integrity, and reviews ingestion pipeline changes ([Cerid-AI/cerid-ai](https://github.com/Cerid-AI/cerid-ai/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/kb-curator.md) · ★3)
- **kb-curator**: Audits KB ingestion quality, checks for duplicate content in ChromaDB/Neo4j, validates graph integrity, and reviews ingestion pipeline changes. Grok-optimized version with strong subagent support for large audits. ([Cerid-AI/cerid-ai](https://github.com/Cerid-AI/cerid-ai/blob/HEAD/.grok/agents/kb-curator.md) · ★3)
- **kb-curator**: Knowledge Base curator agent for periodic deep curation - normalizes tags, discovers relationships, manages topics, detects duplicates, and handles memory lifecycle ([gaurangrshah/gsc-plugins](https://github.com/gaurangrshah/gsc-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/worklog/agents/kb-curator.md))
- **kb-curator**: Use this agent at every Full and Research /close-task, and at a Transitory close when the session produced a report, spec or review, to fold the session's durable knowledge into docs/knowledge_base/. It ingests what the session committed and produced (report, spec, alpha-test result, chronicle tail, diff), applies a notability gate, runs an evidence-cited dedup ladder against the existing KB, and then WRITES directly — but ONLY under docs/knowledge_base/: duplicates are skipped, stale articles are rewritten in place, genuinely new topics become new articles scaffolded from the kb_article temp… ([Zuus89/lifecycle-framework](https://github.com/Zuus89/lifecycle-framework/blob/HEAD/agents/kb-curator.md))
- **kb-data-validator**: Validates a bundled knowledge-base file (data/&lt;patch&gt;/knowledge.json) for structural integrity, full champion coverage, and proper source/confidence tagging. - **kb-researcher**: Searches the REPOSITORY'S COMPILED KNOWLEDGE BASE — prior findings, decisions, gotchas and root causes distilled from previous sessions by the knowledge-compiler. It does NOT read source code, does NOT search the web, and NEVER modifies anything. - **kb-retrieval**: KB retrieval and current-research specialist. Trigger phrases: "answer from the KB", "what do we know about", "tune KB search", "retrieval benchmark", "brief me on", "what's new since", "verify this claim". - **kb-reviewer**: Read-only reviewer of roam KB answers and knowledge-graph quality. - **kb-surveyor**: Survey a repo/module's code and draft anchored knowledge cards (status draft). Used by kb-bootstrap phase B2, fanned out one agent per repo/module. ([mushi310/subfleet](https://github.com/mushi310/subfleet/blob/HEAD/agents/kb-surveyor.md))
- **kb-taxonomy-architect**: Designs the hierarchy for a LaTeX-to-Markdown knowledge base: invariants list, document skeleton, navigation spec, and acceptance criteria. - **kg-context-dispatch**: Pre-flight context dispatcher invoked by the main Claude session before any substantive code or architecture work. Invoke this every time you're about to make a non-trivial code change or design decision. ([potenlab/kg-workflow](https://github.com/potenlab/kg-workflow/blob/HEAD/kg-workflow-plugin/agents/kg-context-dispatch.md))

- **kg-history-check**: Search prior user prompts, decisions, and sessions for context relevant to the current prompt. - **kg-impl-check**: Look up what the Impl KG (`.understand-anything/knowledge-graph.json`) says about the area the user is touching. ([potenlab/kg-workflow](https://github.com/potenlab/kg-workflow/blob/HEAD/kg-workflow-plugin/agents/kg-impl-check.md))

Invoke once per EXPLOITED finding after the report agent completes. - **ki-governance-researcher**: Du bist die **Recherche-Stufe** in der Researcher → Drafter → Reviewer-Pipeline. Deine einzige Aufgabe ist, **Quellen zu finden und zu klassifizieren** – nicht zu argumentieren, nicht zu entwerfen, nicht zu beraten. Du lieferst die organisationsrechtlichen, datenschutzrechtlichen, berufsrechtlichen und standardisierungstechnischen Belege, die der Drafter braucht, um Governance-Artefakte (Risikoass ([borghei/AI-Skills-German-Law](https://github.com/borghei/AI-Skills-German-Law/blob/HEAD/ki-governance/agents/researcher.md) · ★31)
- **ki-governance-reviewer**: Du bist die **Qualitäts- und Risikostufe** vor Auslieferung an die Geschäftsleitung / den Mandantenanwalt. - **kirei-arch**: Architecture research agent. Maps module boundaries, dependency flows, coupling issues, and structural decisions. Produces a Mermaid diagram embedded in the findings doc (renders natively on GitHub) plus written architectural findings. Advisory only — no code changes. - **kirei-bundle**: Bundle-size research agent. Measures the actual built output, identifies the heaviest contributors, finds duplicate dependencies, missing code splits, unshaken tree leaves, and bloated assets. Distinct from kirei-perf (broad runtime bottleneck mapping) — this agent owns shipped-bytes deeply. Produces a structured handoff with measurable byte savings for kirei-stitch or kirei-loom. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-bundle.md))
- **kirei-data**: Data layer research agent. Audits database schemas, migrations, queries, and indexes for safety, correctness, and design quality. Distinct from kirei-perf — focuses on schema design, migration safety, and data-integrity, not just query speed. Produces a structured handoff for kirei-stitch or kirei-loom. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-data.md))
- **kirei-debug**: Debug research agent. Reproduces a specific bug, traces the failure to its root cause, and prescribes a targeted fix. May add temporary instrumentation (logs, asserts) during diagnosis — every instrumentation point is tracked and listed in the handoff so kirei-stitch / kirei-loom can remove it after the real fix lands. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-debug.md))
- **kirei-deps**: Dependency-safety research agent. Detects the project's package manager, runs its audit, fetches GitHub Dependabot alerts (if available), and produces a depth-tunable report — quick CVE counts, safe-bump list, or full ordered upgrade plan. Distinct from kirei-security (broader codebase audit) and kirei-migrate (single-package version bump). Produces a structured handoff for kirei-stitch, or for kirei-migrate if a risky major bump is needed. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-deps.md))
- **kirei-eval**: Evaluation-infrastructure research agent. Audits how the project measures whether its own code (or its AI/LLM features) actually works — eval suites, benchmark harnesses, regression baselines, golden datasets, snapshot strategies, A/B test infra. Distinct from kirei-test (unit/integration coverage) — this agent owns the meta question "do we know when quality regresses?". Produces a structured handoff for kirei-stitch or kirei-loom. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-eval.md))
- **kirei-gate**: Adversarial, read-only merge-gate reviewer. Independently reviews a PR or diff range against its stated intent, hunts for what CI cannot see (injection reachable from config, path traversal, command-exec sinks, trust-boundary changes, secrets in logs, destructive migrations, and silent reverts of prior fixes), and ends with exactly one line — `VERDICT: MERGE` or `VERDICT: HOLD`. Structurally read-only (no Edit/Write) and non-interactive (no AskUserQuestion) so it is safe to run in the background as a gate. Distinct from kirei-review (interactive, writes a findings doc, triages comments) — kir… ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-gate.md))
- **kirei-license**: License-compatibility research agent. Audits dependencies for license types, flags incompatible combinations (GPL contagion against proprietary code, AGPL in network-served code, missing NOTICE/attribution requirements), checks the project's own LICENSE file matches reality, and produces a remediation plan. Distinct from kirei-deps (CVE focus) and kirei-security (broader audit). Produces a structured handoff for kirei-stitch. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-license.md))
- **kirei-loom**: Use this agent to implement findings from a kirei research agent for complex tasks. - **kirei-migrate**: Migration research agent. - **kirei-observability**: Observability research agent. Audits logs, metrics, and traces for coverage, structure, and safety. Finds missing instrumentation in critical paths, error swallowing, log-level inconsistency, PII / secret leakage in logs, missing correlation IDs, and unstructured logging. Distinct from kirei-perf (latency causes) and kirei-security (broader codebase). Produces a structured handoff for kirei-stitch or kirei-loom. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-observability.md))
- **kirei-perf**: Performance research agent. Investigates bundle size, render bottlenecks, N+1 queries, memory leaks, cache misses, and latency hotspots. - **kirei-refactor**: Refactoring research agent. Identifies code smells, dead code, duplication, abstraction violations, and structural debt. ([Shironex/kirei](https://github.com/Shironex/kirei/blob/HEAD/agents/kirei-refactor.md))
- **kirei-research**: Use this agent when you need to research, investigate, or analyze a codebase problem before implementing a fix. - **kirei-resilience**: Error-handling research agent. - **kirei-review**: Code review research agent. Reviews pending local changes OR a GitHub PR (via `gh`), surfaces real issues, and produces a structured report. - **kirei-security**: Security-focused research agent. Audits for OWASP Top 10, auth flows, secrets exposure, dependency vulnerabilities, injection points, and access control issues. - **kirei-sentry**: Sentry integration agent.
